The target should not be “100M memorizes code.” The target should be “100M uses compact repo-grounded evidence well enough to make correct software decisions, while KBPP/retrieval supplies exact facts and code spans.”\n\nA good software developer AI rubric should measure these layers:\n\n1. Repository Grounding\n- Can locate relevant files, symbols, tests, configs, imports, and call sites.\n- Can distinguish same-name symbols in different modules.\n- Can cite the evidence it used.\n- Fails if it guesses without repo evidence.\n\n2. Syntax And Code Construction\n- Produces valid syntax.\n- Preserves formatting/style conventions.\n- Uses existing APIs and patterns.\n- Avoids inventing unavailable functions/classes.\n\n3. Dependency Understanding\n- Understands imports, module boundaries, package layout, config files, environment assumptions.\n- Knows when a change requires updating tests, docs, schemas, migrations, type hints, or downstream callers.\n- Detects hidden coupling between files.\n\n4. Operation Semantics\n- Understands what each statement/expression does.\n- Can infer exact expressions from local context when enough evidence exists.\n- Can explain why one implementation is behaviorally different from another.\n- Handles edge cases, error paths, async behavior, mutation, state, and I/O.\n\n5. Change Impact\n- Predicts affected systems inside the repo.\n- Finds callers and tests impacted by a change.\n- Avoids breaking public APIs unless explicitly required.\n- Understands compatibility and migration risk.\n\n6. Verification\n- Selects or writes relevant tests.\n- Runs or proposes correct checks.\n- Interprets failures.\n- Does not claim success without evidence.\n\n7. Repair Ability\n- Given failing tests/logs, identifies root cause.\n- Makes minimal correct patch.\n- Avoids broad unrelated rewrites.\n- Verifies fix.\n\n8. Implementation Quality\n- Simple, maintainable code.\n- Matches repo style.\n- Uses existing abstractions.\n- Avoids overengineering.\n- Handles errors explicitly.\n\n9. Exactness\n- For code generation: exact expression/statement/function match where benchmark requires it.\n- For real development: behavioral equivalence matters more than text exactness.\n- KBPP should copy exact spans when exactness matters; the model should select/verify.\n\n10. Honesty And Uncertainty\n- Knows when context is insufficient.\n- Requests broader retrieval instead of hallucinating.\n- Separates evidence, inference, and guess.\n\nBased on our training so far, the immediate missing pieces are:\n\n- Repo-local retrieval, not global expression pools.\n- Candidate spans from same file, sibling functions, call sites, imports, tests, and configs.\n- A selector/verifier objective over repo-derived candidates.\n- Impact-analysis datasets: “if this changes, what else changes?”\n- Test-grounded repair tasks, not just expression reconstruction.\n- Benchmarks that measure full patch success, not only exact expression selection.\n\nThe practical rubric score could be:\n\n- 20% repo retrieval/grounding\n- 15% syntax and API correctness\n- 15% semantic operation understanding\n- 15% change impact analysis\n- 15% test/verification behavior\n- 10% exact code span selection/composition\n- 10% implementation quality and uncertainty handling\n\nFor our next training step, I’d prioritize repo-local candidate retrieval and selector/verifier training.   165\t.. rubric:: Example: 1D TMA load of two buffers with arrival token (sm90+)\n   166\t\n   167\tThe following example shows how to use TMA to load two tiles of data from global memory into shared memory:\n   168\t\n   169\t.. code:: cuda\n   170\t\n   171\t   #include <cuda/barrier>\n   172\t   #include <cuda/ptx>\n   173\t\n   174\t   // selects a single leader thread from the block\n   175\t   __device__ bool elect_one() {\n   176\t     const unsigned int tid = threadIdx.x;\n   177\t     const unsigned int warp_id = tid / 32;\n   178\t     const unsigned int uniform_warp_id = __shfl_sync(0xFFFFFFFF, warp_id, 0); // broadcast from lane 0\n   179\t     return uniform_warp_id == 0 && cuda::ptx::elect_sync(0xFFFFFFFF); // elect a leader thread among warp 0\n   180\t   }\n   181\t\n   182\t   __global__ void example_kernel(int* gmem1, double* gmem2) {\n   183\t     constexpr int tile_size = 1024;\n   184\t     __shared__ alignas(16)    int smem1[tile_size];\n   185\t 217\tinto the shared memory buffers ``smem1`` and ``smem2``.\n   218\tThe shared memory buffers have to be aligned to 16 bytes.\n   219\tAdditionally, a single barrier with block scope is setup by a The schema is educational first: it should teach what the concept is, why it exists, how builders use it, and where it fails.\\n\\n## Required Fields\\n\\n- Concept\\n- One-sentence intuition\\n- Formal definition\\n- Prerequisites\\n- What problem it solves\\n- Minimal equation\\n- Common implementation\\n- Failure modes\\n- Canonical papers\\n- Related Atlas nodes\\n- Exercise\\n- What to learn next\\n\\n## Failure Modes Are Required\\n\\nFailure modes are not optional decoration. They make the Atlas useful for builders.\\n\\nExamples:\\n\\n- Attention: quadratic memory, softmax saturation, position handling, masking mistakes, KV-cache cost.\\n- Diffusion: sampling cost, schedule sensitivity, guidance artifacts, mode coverage issues.\\n- RAG: retrieval mismatch, stale index, citation hallucination, chunking failure.\\n- Agents: tool error compounding, goal drift, hidden state ambiguity, eval brittleness.\\n\\n## Edge Explanation Fields\\n\\nEvery displayed dependency edge should eventually expose:\\n\\n- Source concept\\n- Target concept\\n- Type\\n- Strength\\n- Why this dependency exists\\n- Skip consequence\\n\\nExample:\\n\\nTransformer -> Attention\\n\\nType: composes\\nStrength: core\\nWhy: Transformers replace recurrence-heavy sequence processing with stacked attention and feed-forward blocks. Without attention, the architecture no longer has its defining token-token interaction mechanism.\\nSkip consequence: If you skip attention, Transformer diagrams become vocabulary without mechanism.\\n''',\\n'TRUST_LEVELS.md': '''# Atlas Trust Levels\\n\\nThe Atlas is aiming for complete AI coverage, but coverage and verification are separate.
